ParlaMint-PL_2018-09-12-sejm-68-1.u16	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! I present a report by the Extraordinary Commission on amendments to the codifications of the Government draft law amending the Code of Offences Act and certain other laws, the printing of Seym No 2557. Marshal of the Sejm at the 63rd meeting of the Sejm on 7 June 2018 pursuant to Article. 87. 2 in conjunction with Article. 90. 1 of the Rules of Procedure of the Sejm referred the above draft law to the Extraordinary Commission for amendments to the codifications for its consideration. The Commission, after considering this proposal at its meetings on 6 and 17 July 2018, requests the High Sejm to adopt the attached draft law. The Extraordinary Commission has appointed a subcommittee to consider this project. The subcommittee amended the article. 118 § 1, Art. 119 § 1 and 2, Art. 122 § 1 and Article 1 124 § 1 Code of misdemeanors, i.e. with regard to the acquisition of property, intentional and unintentional fences, and the extraction and logging of wood. The amount was raised, the value of the object of the prohibited act: from 400 PLN - so was the project - to 500 PLN. This amount is a cesarean, is the limit value for offences and crimes against property. This amount was considered to be more appropriate to the level of revenue at present. The second amendment concerns a new definition of continuous act by adding it to Article. 12 Criminal Code § 2 excluding the grounds for committing an act of preconceived intent. The third amendment concerns Article. Ten misdemeanor codes. The following article is inserted: 10a with a new content, clarifying the cases permitting the execution of penalties and the application of criminal measures for offences which it provides, in conjunction with other acts, in view of the total value of the property of the offence and introducing the principle that, in the event of a penalty or a criminal measure of the same kind, a stricter penalty or a stricter criminal measure shall be imposed. The fourth major change concerns vacathio legis. The basic 14-day deadline for the entry into force of this law was maintained in the case of a vast majority of the provisions, whereas in the case of an electronic record of offences it is a period of 12 months. A period of 6 months has also been introduced for the adoption of a regulation by the President of the Council of Ministers on the imposition of fines in the form of a criminal mandate and on the amendment of the regulation of the Council of Ministers on the procedure for forms of criminal mandate. High Seym! On behalf of the Extraordinary Commission for Changes in Codifications, I request that this draft law be adopted. Thank you very much. ParlaMint-PL_2018-09-12-sejm-68-1.u18	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice, I support the draft law amending the Code of Offences and certain other laws, Seym Printing No. 2557 and 2631, in particular as regards the amendment of the provisions on the determination of the limit value of damage determining the classification of theft as a criminal offence by accepting the amount of PLN 500, recognising that this is a more quantitative solution than the reference of a fraction to the minimum wage, as it has been since 2013. I know that this salary changes every year; from next year the minimum rate will be PLN 2250. The bill also provides for legal provisions allowing the perpetrator to be free from the second type of substitute penalty, carried out in the form of useful work, by paying the amount of money still to be paid. Changes in the provisions of the Code of Conduct in cases of misconduct concern the improvement of the procedure, the visibility of the role of the mandate procedure as the most frequently used procedure. The definition of continuous misconduct is also introduced in Article. 10 § 1. There have also been amendments to other laws, namely the Law of 20 April 2004 on the promotion of employment and the institutions of the labour market. These changes typize in a new way, among others. offences under Article. Article 119 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 2 of this law: The unemployed person who has taken up employment, other employment or economic activity without informing the competent district labour office shall be fined. This solution departs from the solution so far, from setting the lower limit of the fine at the level of PLN 500, which should be considered too restrictive for the unemployed person so far. The article was also amended. 120. 2 of this bill. It received a new wording: "A foreigner who is illegally engaged in work shall be fined. This gives him the opportunity to be punished directly in the term of office. Similarly, the Article has been amended. Paragraph 122. 1 Act on employment promotion in the case of persons who commit offences without fulfilling their obligation to pay contributions to the Labour Fund or not paying them within the prescribed time limits. The mandate procedure was also subject to a breach concerning the failure to notify the required data or to report false data affecting the level of contributions to the Labour Fund, or to give false explanations in this respect or to refuse to grant them. The draft law also sets the maximum limit for expenditure of the State budget resulting from the financial entry into force of this law - an amount of 28 891 thousand. zł - for the years 2018 to 2027, and it should be stressed here that this will mainly concern the introduction of an electronic register of offenders and a new IT system in general. The Law and Justice Club supports this project and requests its adoption. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2018-09-12-sejm-68-1.u20	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour to present, on behalf of the Parliamentary Club, the Civic Platform's position on the committee's report on the draft law amending the Code of Offences Act. In general, the project concerns changes in the scope of so-called. half-life, the establishment of a police record of offences, changes in the mandate procedure and tightening of rules on the holding of dangerous animals. Both substantive and editorial amendments were made during the work of the subcommittee and committee. In the current legal situation, half-off acts constitute a misdemeanor if the value of the goods does not exceed 1/4 of the minimum wage. Today the project developer proposes to return to a fixed amount and after work in the commission shows a cesarea of PLN 500; initially the project assumed PLN 400. In justifying the amendment, the committee points out that this amount is more appropriate to the revenue generated today. Of course, there are supporters and opponents of the increase in this amount, which was also evident during the work of the committee. Certainly, the proposed changes will still be discussed on a number of occasions, as will the way in which the halved acts are regulated. The Commission also proposes a new definition of a continuous act - the exclusion of one of the grounds for committing an act with a preconceived intention. Another amendment concerns the clarification of the cases for which penalties and criminal measures may be imposed for offences which it constitutes, together with other acts, because of the total value of the property of the offence and introduces the principle that in the event of a penalty or a criminal measure of the same kind, the most severe penalty shall be enforced. The Commission has also proposed to extend the vacathio legis of certain provisions. These are the most important substantive amendments proposed by the committee. As a platform, we will be in favour of them. Thank you.

ParlaMint-PL_2018-09-12-sejm-68-1.u24	Dear Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! On behalf of the club of the Polish People's Party - Union of European Democrats, I am pleased to present my position towards the government's draft law amending the Code of Offences and some other laws, prints No. 2557 and No. 2631. Ladies and gentlemen! First of all, the project proposes a change in the rule by which it determines whether the theft committed is still a crime or already a crime. It's a big difference, because a criminal offence is most often punishable by a fine, and even five years in prison for the theft. According to the current regulation, this limit sets the value of damage of 1/4 of the minimum wage for work, i.e. currently 525 PLN. The project originally proposed that the limit should be a fixed amount of PLN 400 and any theft of objects of value above that amount would be a crime. During the work in the subcommittee, this fixed value was increased to PLN 500. At the stage of the subcommittee's work, a modification of the Penal Code was added to the project, according to which: Responsible as one prohibited act for the exhaustive character of the offence, who, at short intervals, on the same or the same occasion, or in a similar manner commits two or more intentional offences against property, if the total value of the property justifies the responsibility for the crime. The draft also contains changes to the deadlines: from 7 to 14 days the deadline for paying the mandate is to be extended, e.g. for traffic offences registered by a photo-radar, incorrect parking or not snowing of the sidewalk at home. It has also been proposed, ladies and gentlemen, to tighten the penalties for failing to protect dangerous animals. For example, one of the laws now being amended is: Whoever does not take the usual or prescribed precautions when holding an animal is punishable by a fine of up to 250 zlotys or reprimands. The amendment proposes raising the fine to 1 000 in this provision. zł and the possibility of a penalty for the restriction of freedom has been added. In certain cases, the fine can reach up to 5 000. zł. In addition, it is planned to establish an electronic register of offenders against property, persons suspected of committing such offences, blamed and punished. The register will be used by the police, prosecutors and courts to ensure that thieves who commit petty thefts in different cities are not responsible for individual offenses, but for a summarized crime. Ladies and gentlemen! The Club of the Polish People's Party - the European Union of Democrats will support the proposed solutions. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2018-09-12-sejm-68-1.u32	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Poselski Club Modern I submit my position on the government draft law amending the Code of Offences Act and some other laws, prints No. 2557 and 2631. The proposed project aims to amend the so-called regulation. half-deeds. In addition, it regulates the establishment of a police record of offences, changes in the mandate procedure, and tightening of rules on the holding of dangerous animals. The way of regulating the half-deeds has long been discussed in doctrine. Depending on whether the offence constitutes a criminal offence or an offence, the value of the subject matter of the enforcement action, at the same time the variable threshold of that liability, depending on the minimum remuneration for the year in question, was intended to achieve those values. The project submitted assumes a return to a rigid border. Originally, it was proposed that the Cesare should amount to PLN 400, but the subcommittee introduced an amendment to raise this amount to PLN 500. The project also provides for the introduction of additional powers for the Police in the form of the creation of a central record of offences, in fact a database of offenders, as well as suspected offenders and victims. This is an undoubted step to facilitate the fight against the so-called. thieves with calculator in their hand, i.e. with persons stealing goods of value only up to the threshold amount. However, there are doubts about the compatibility of this project with GDPR regulations. It should also be noted that the proposed amendment of the deadline for the imposition of a mandate for 60 days will in some cases lead to an extension of that deadline. These dates are currently different - 14, 90 and 180 days respectively - depending on how the perpetrator is disclosed. In the event that the perpetrator of the offence was caught in the act or immediately after the offence was committed, that period has so far been 14 days. The extension of up to 60 days in this area should be negatively assessed. The proposal to tighten the rules on the holding of dangerous animals deserves to be commended. Numerous cases of improper handling of dangerous animals prove that there is a need for change in this respect. The proposed proposal waives the definition of a minimum fine for offences involving employment, including illegal work by foreigners. This gives the impression of an ad hoc response to labour market demand, including seasonal work. However, there is no systemic action here, and such actions should be required from the rulers. Despite my doubts, it must be acknowledged that this project is moving in the right direction. Therefore, Members and Members of Modernity will vote in favour of it.

ParlaMint-PL_2018-09-12-sejm-68-1.u34	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Committee on Agriculture and Rural Development, I present a report on the government draft law on farmers' cooperatives, print No 1425. The Commission proposes that the Sejm adopt the attached bill. As far as farmers' cooperatives are concerned, this problem, which has not yet been resolved, was already in 2004 a similar bill, slightly amended, to be sent to the Sejm. Then Mr. Belka's government took a negative stance on the parliamentary printing. Then, already in the years 2005-2007, we took similar action on the adoption of the Farmers' Cooperatives Act, but as we know, in 2007 that term was interrupted. In 2008, our club, Law and Justice, submitted a draft law on farmers' cooperatives. Unfortunately, the ruling coalition did not want to bring this into legal order. This was referred to the Constitutional Committee. The coalition's position was negative, too. Only in this term of office will the bill, I think, be passed. I'm presenting the committee's report today. It took quite a long time to work in committee because the bill required and required notification. The notification itself lasted almost eight months. From the moment the number was given to this day, and the number was given on 22 March 2017, almost 1.5 years have passed and today we are considering this law. The Commission shall, after considering this draft and approving it or adopting the rules resulting from the notification, present this report. The Farmers' Cooperatives Act lays down rules for the creation, organisation, activity and liquidation of farmers' cooperatives and their associations. It is a law which must be compatible in the sense of the application of the law with the cooperative law of 1982. This is, you can say, a special act for farmers, which will enable economic activity to be activated in rural areas, in the countryside. As we know, in the Polish legal system there are mainly capital companies or producer groups, and farmers' cooperatives, these typical ones, so far, have not functioned so much to the extent that this law provides for. This is where we introduce in the article. 6 that farmers' cooperatives are engaged in economic activities in favour of farmers' cooperatives' members in planning and adapting their production of products or groups of products to market conditions, concentrating supply and organising the sale of products or groups of products produced by farmers, concentrating demand and organising the purchase of the necessary inputs to produce products or groups of products to farmers. Cooperatives in addition to carrying out the activities referred to in paragraph 1. 1, may carry out activities in the field of, i.e. those necessary activities - the storage, packaging and standardisation of products or groups of products produced by farmers, the processing of products or groups of products produced by farmers, the provision to farmers of services related to the production of products or groups of products, the sale of products or groups of products, the dissemination to their members of environmentally beneficial cultivation methods and production technology, the pursuit of service activities for their members. Founders of cooperatives - this is also important - can be farmers whose number cannot be less than 10. The rules for the operation and economy of farmers' cooperatives shall be laid down in a statute which shall be drawn up on the basis of the Cooperative Law, but also on the basis of the provisions which are contained in that law. A very important issue is chapter 6, which provides that, in order to stimulate the development of common economic structures in the form of farmers' cooperatives and the creation of agricultural networks, several laws are being amended. Firstly, the Civil Code amends the fact that the contracting agreements between an agricultural producer and an agricultural producer also refer to the cooperative of farmers or the association of such cooperatives. The Act on inheritance tax and donations amends the tax exemption for the acquisition of rights to contributions to farmers' cooperatives, the Act on local taxes and charges in respect of the exemption from property tax, buildings and buildings or parts thereof and land which are owned or permanently used by farmers' cooperatives, the Act on income tax on natural persons as regards the adjustment of the rules in force to the proposed new legal institution concerning the possibility to dispose of shares in farmers' cooperatives, the Act on corporation tax as regards the adjustment of the rules in force to the proposed new legal institution concerning the possibility to dispose of shares, as regards the exemption from tax on the income of farmers' cooperatives operating as a micro-company from the sale of agricultural products or groups of such products or fish. And what is important is that the Act on the Agency for Restructuring and Modernization of Agriculture indicates that one of the tasks carried out by the Agency is to support the formation and development of farmers' cooperatives and their associations. Like I said, the bill was notified by the government. During the work of the Civic Platform committee, he announced that there would be amendments. Of course, the committee will consider these amendments. We are here together for this law, when it comes into force, to be the best instrument for the organisation of processing and trade in rural areas, but also for the concentration and cooperation of farmers. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

Yes, we can recall the second historical area, perhaps even more affecting the mentality of the older generation of farmers, but strongly rooted in the Polish tradition, in peasant farms. This is a period of perversion of cooperatives, a post-war period, when under the banner of cooperatives, breaking all the rules, European cooperative rules established in Vienna were called cooperatives in Poland, something which as a living had nothing to do with this noble, beautiful, elevated cooperative movement in Europe. So the soviet-like collectiveization of agriculture after the temporary transfer of land to farmers in the form of agricultural reform 1944, then the forced collectiveisation carried out in a brutal way, the ruthless way that led, also as part of the fight against peasant movement, to murders, to prisons, and people, crying, entered production cooperatives called with Russian collectives, collective chasiaiities. Ten, almost 10,000. production cooperatives, which were formed in Poland after the war, in one month after Stalin's death, when the freedom of association was allowed, 9 thousand were dissolved. cooperative, in one month. That means it was an idea deeply incomprehensible and unacceptable by farmers. Unfortunately, this was the so-called state of affairs. Cooperatives influence the thinking of farmers, also this period after the collectiveisation, when cooperative societies operating in rural areas, I am not only talking about agricultural cooperatives, but also about other forms of cooperatives, it was a de facto extension of state power, it was part of the distribution of rare goods, because in socialism everything was rare: there was a shortage of coal, lack of feed, lack of fertilizer, lack of basic means of production, lack of machinery, lack of tractors, lack of everything. The cooperative was the intermediary who, for the chosen, for those who may have been more on the basis of a line, provided access to these rare goods. She was often associated with pathology rather than good organization of work or activity. Telling how much someone has in the GS, how much will it cost, etc., did not come from anything, so the dislike of farmers is at the moment an unjustified charge of cooperativeism, but somewhere deeply rooted in this historical consciousness of Poles. Now if we are convinced - and I have not heard the voices of opposition - that cooperatives should be developed that farmers need to be encouraged not only because the mythical European Union will give additional money, it will be for a moment, for a short time, the reporting requirement and such entities, as experience shows, are failing, not for the ad hoc benefit of some money transfers, but for this market position, which is increasingly difficult, which during this period, during globalisation, at a time - we have just talked about this in committee - the domination of powerful trading networks, the domination of large processing plants that consolidate and become even greater, the dominance of intermediaries on the market... Only and only farmers' cooperation can give an opportunity - it does not give that certainty, but it can give a chance - that organised farmers can be a link that will not be eaten, will not be dominated by those stronger market participants. So getting to consciousness, however, is, in my opinion, a more important element than just another money, not always well spent. There is an example of fruit and vegetable groups. In Europe, agricultural cooperatives are a widespread form of farmers' association. According to statistics, there are around 22 000 in the European Union. agricultural production cooperatives which have more than 50% participation in the supply of agricultural inputs and more than 60% in the concentration, processing and marketing of agricultural products. An example should be agricultural cooperatives in France, where many important agri-food companies have been taking over by cooperatives for several years and the creation of marketing and processing branches. Farmers control more than half of the agricultural processing industry in France through cooperatives and subsidiaries. There is also an increasing involvement of farmers and cooperatives in the production of renewable energy, which is encouraged by legislation which is lacking in Polish legislation. Another example - Italy. In Italy almost 6,000 were already in operation 10 years ago. cooperatives from different agricultural sectors: most services, then dairy, fruit and vegetable, meat and wine-producing cooperatives. Germany has also created excellent conditions for the development of cooperatives, in particular energy. Between 2006 and 2014, renewable energy cooperatives themselves were created there more than 700. Meanwhile, in Poland on an area of about 230 thousand. ha agricultural area is carried out by less than 700 agricultural production cooperatives. In foreign hands there are buying points, fruit and vegetable processing plants, dairy houses and slaughterhouses, which dictate prices on Polish agricultural products. We're directly dependent on foreign capital. We lack sovereignty in the most crucial economic division for each country. It's too late for round words, no concrete and superficial changes. Today, the government is spending millions on social aid, boasting of lively consumption, growing pillars of sales of goods. It is said that the real beneficiaries of social programmes are commercial - foreign networks. Foreign capital essentially takes over most of the proceeds from commercial networks. Over the past few years, Polish grocery stores have gradually disappeared so that only a handful of them will be present. In view of the above, the measure of effectiveness of the contested law should not be the creation of several dozen new cooperatives, but the recovery of individual components of the supply chain, i.e. processing and trade. This is the objective that we should set before us, and this is the purpose of subjecting the provisions of the bill to it. I ask the Minister for such solutions for Polish agriculture.

The proposed law aims to ensure transparency in the business activities carried out by members of the family of labour inspectors, in so far as it relates to the inspection activities carried out by those inspectors in order to avoid conflicts of interest. On 5 September 2018, a joint meeting of the Commission for State Control and the Committee on Social Policy and Family Policy took place on the first reading and examination of the draft law and an amendment on the Article was adopted. 3, i.e. the date of entry into force of this law. ParlaMint-PL_2018-09-12-sejm-68-1.u104	Thank you very much. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, the Civic Platform has the honour to present its position on amending the Act on State Labour Inspection, print No 2351. High Izbo! In accordance with the proposed amendment, an inspection officer, i.e. a labour inspector, will have to make a written statement on the conduct or absence by the nearest person within the meaning of Article. 115 § 11 of the Code of Criminal Business Activity, the object of which coincides with the subject matter of the audit carried out at the company. It is therefore about informing about the business activity carried out by a very wide range of people, i.e. the spouse, the parent, the child, the siblings, the relative in the same line or degree, the person remaining in the relationship of adoption and her spouse and the person remaining in a common relationship with the labour inspector. The aim of the revision of the conflict of interests of labour inspectors must, of course, be supported. The Civic Platform supports all actions, including legislative initiatives aimed at eliminating or reducing corruption. However, the project is not entirely clear and it is worse when it comes to details. First of all, Art. 115 § 11 of the Penal Code defines a very wide circle of persons. Hence, the question arises as to whether it is too wide for revision. Will the inspectors not have a problem with the reliable and truthful provision of the information required? After all, the labour inspector may simply not know about the activities carried out by persons of his family or other persons closest to him within the meaning of Article. 115 § 11 of the Penal Code and thus expose themselves to negative consequences from the employer. On this aspect the amendment also draws attention to the government's position. In addition, the question arises as to whether the current Article. The 48th Law on State Labour Inspection prohibiting employees from carrying out any activity which is contrary to the interests of the State Labour Inspection or which does not comply with its tasks, and which is contrary to the obligations of the employee or which may give rise to suspicion of bias or interest, is not sufficient in this respect. It is also not clear what content the declaration to be made by labour inspectors will have. Wouldn't it be appropriate for the project to include details of this statement and not leave the freedom of the Chief Labour Inspector with regard to its content? At least some basic scope of information that will be provided in it should be specified by law. Or is it even worth trying to make a model statement like that? Finally, it is extremely important: a circle of people affected by the amendment. Why should the statements be made only by labour inspectors? The Act on State Labour Inspection distinguishes between the two in the article. 38 persons carrying out control activities, i.e. labour inspectors, from persons supervising the inspection, i.e. the chief labour inspector and his deputies, as well as district labour inspectors and their deputies. Shouldn't they also make such statements? This may be very important in particular when considering appeals against decisions of labour inspectors or decisions of district labour inspectors. The transparency of actions should apply to all those involved in the supervisory process. I don't think the government should have anything to hide, either, as it is required of its subordinates. As I said earlier, the Civic Platform supports all measures aimed at eliminating corruption phenomena, but the proposed amendment needs to be clarified, and therefore, on behalf of the parliamentary club, I am proposing amendments to the procedated project. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2018-09-12-sejm-68-1.u106	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour to present the position of the Modern Club regarding the proposed amendment to the Act on State Labour Inspection. It is the printing number 2351 and the printing number 2814 adding the article. 44a in the sound indicated there. We see intentions, but we have some legitimate doubts. Namely, the proposed provision does not introduce any effective mechanism to protect against the possibility of corrupt behaviour, because it cannot be considered as such to introduce an obligation to declare, to this demanding form both positive and negative. By the way, this will apply to practically all employees of the State Labour Inspection performing control activities. Moreover, regulation creates only a bureaucratic obligation which does not translate into any form of liability by the person making such a declaration. To date, the statutory regulation referred to in Article 2 of Regulation (EU) No 182/2011 shall apply. Article 44 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 2 that the staff carrying out the control activities cannot participate in the interests of the control entities remains a clear condition for the assessment of the legality of the control body's activities, which in each case must be assessed individually in the facts in question. In addition, the regulation omits a completely different group of persons carrying out tasks within the State Labour Inspection, namely persons supervising control activities also having control powers which are not bound by the provision of that Act. This means that people employed in the Chief Labour Inspectors, including executives, and district labour inspectors, e.g. Assistants or district labour inspectors are not covered by this obligation, which leaves this professional group most vulnerable to corruption temptations outside the obligation to strengthen protection mechanisms against corruption. In addition to any doubt, it is also too enigmatic to mention the content of the statement which must be made to the fact that the person closest to the undertaking is engaged in or not carrying out an economic activity whose subject-matter coincides with the subject-matter of the inspection. The labour inspector shall carry out about nine inspections within a month. So is the analysis of his professional activity for the submission of a statement to be the subject of any activity in this regard? This would lead to an absurd bureaucratic burden, which remains, as I mentioned above, without consequences. The credibility of this statement will be quite easy to challenge due to the circle of actors which the legislator covers the obligation to declare the person carrying out the inspection. The obligation to make a declaration in accordance with the draft shall apply to the nearest person within the meaning of Article. 115 § 11 of the Penal Code, i.e. the spouse, the first, second, siblings, the same line or degree, the person in the relationship of adoption and her spouse, and the person in the common life. Already the circle of actors itself excludes the possibility of making an effective statement, since the person making the declaration will have the opportunity to obtain information about the spouse of the person being appointed? It is also against the law to create responsibilities for those living together. This creates the possibility of unlawful interference with the private life of the person performing the control and in addition may result in the imposition of an obligation which cannot be effectively achieved, is the person making the declaration, nor will it be possible to verify such information. The mere identification of the circle of entities on which there is an obligation to declare constitutes a paracarinal procedure by linking them to criminal law rules. Apart from the provisions of the Act, which, through the temporary training mechanism of the Labour Inspector and the analysis of his social attitudes, including cyclical work assessments, allows to presume the integrity and transparency of the action of the State Labour Inspection Authority itself, and the cases of infringements in this area remain incidental and dealt with in internal procedures for this purpose, i.e. disciplinary and disciplinary. In conclusion, two points need to be addressed. Firstly, the training process of the labour inspector is complex, is spread over time, allows to assess the candidate not only from the point of view of his qualifications, but also moral and ethical attitudes, so important in the work of a public official. Secondly, everyone takes a vow before receiving an inspector's card, [[♪ The bell ♪]] the issue of the rule of law and ethics in professional activities remains an important element. Therefore, there is no reason for the draft wording of the new provision to be drawn up - from this proposal only a bureaucratic dimension can be read - the Modern Club supports its implementation in this way. Thank you. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2018-09-12-sejm-68-1.u121	Thank you very much. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear five minutes' statements on behalf of clubs and circles. I'm opening a discussion. I ask Mrs Józef Hrynkiewicz to speak on behalf of the Law and Justice Club.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-09-12-sejm-68-1.u122	Mrs. Marshal! High Seym! I have the honour to present the position of the Law and Justice Club regarding the Act on the Postgraduate Medical Training Center, the printing of Sejm VIII of the term No. 2548. The draft law regulates the legal statute of the CMKP, an institution that has been operating for 47 years, i.e. Since 1971, on the basis of the Regulation of 9 October 1970. Unregulated for 47 years, the legal status has significantly hindered the fulfilment of the functions for which the CMKP has been established. The law on which we are proceeding today adapts the legal regulations determining the formal status of the CMKP to the current legal situation, including the Law on Higher Education, which enters into force on 1 October 2018. The Postgraduate Medical Training Centre was established as a separate scientific and teaching institution. In the process of developing health care, the medical centre took over the tasks of education and improvement in new medical professions. The Act sets out the tasks of a medical centre in the field of organisation and coordination of post-graduate medical training, defines the organisational structure and competence of a newly established institution which is an important institution in the national post-graduate training system of doctors, dentists, pharmacists, laboratory diagnostics, physiotherapists, paramedics and training in other professions applicable to health care where higher education is required. The establishment and development of new professions requires the establishment of a legally regulated establishment with precise tasks and competences in the field of post-graduate education. The bill assumes that the Postgraduate Medical Training Centre will function as a state legal entity under the name of the Postgraduate Medical Training Centre. The primary area of activity of the institution shall include postgraduate training in the field of medical sciences, health sciences and physical culture, scientific research and development in this field, doctoral studies and postgraduate studies in the field of medical sciences and health and physical culture. The CMKP will continue to be supervised by the Minister of Health Just like in the case of medical schools. The draft law applied provisions similar to those applicable to medical universities. The legal solutions introduced are intended to allow for the development of a modern teaching and scientific centre. The funding of the CMKP will continue to be based on the state budget. The funds for the functioning of the institutions are to come from the subjective grant of the Minister of Health from the third degree study area. In addition, the medical centre may, according to the proposed rules, generate its own revenue from activities involving European funds. The Act allows business activities to be carried out to the extent permitted by public institutions, which is intended to facilitate the provision of paid training. The institution shall retain the old name. The provisions of the Act are adapted to the Act on Higher Education which enters into force on 1 October. The new Act shall enter into force on 1 January 2019. In order to maintain the continuity of the facility, some solutions will be maintained concerning, among others, the operation of the facility. Council of the Scientific Council until the end of its term of office, which began in 2017. The CMKP's statutory amendments provide hope for the development of a facility that will expand its research, development and teaching functions in line with the growing needs of post-graduate education. The solutions applied in the Act provide the legal basis for regulating the status of the institution and adapting it to new solutions in higher education and increasing specialisation needs in medical professions. The Law and Justice Club will vote in favour of the bill as submitted to the Health Commission of 11 September 2018. Thank you so much for your attention. The main objective of the Centre's activities has been for almost 50 years to educate and improve in the professional medical knowledge of doctors, dentists, pharmacists, laboratory diagnostics, paramedics and other persons with a diploma in higher education whose professions are applicable in health care, excluding post-graduate training of nurses and midwives. The Centre, in addition to coordination and post-graduate training in the above mentioned period. medical professions carry out scientific activities, carry out scientific and research work. As a general rule of the draft law, it has been accepted that, in the field not regulated by the proposed law, the provisions of the Law of 20 July 2018 will apply to the Postgraduate Medical Training Centre. may not be applied directly to the CMKP. The bodies of the Centre shall be the Director and the Scientific Council. Authorities will be elected for a term of office on a similar basis to those used in medical institutions. The draft law is intended to maintain the existing arrangements for post-graduate training in the professions applicable to health care. The contested draft law ignores the basic issues relating to the organisation of the centre. The technique of recourse to the provisions of the Law on Higher Education and Science adopted throughout the project is not entirely clear. The fact that the Director of the Centre performs the tasks set out in the above Act does not mean that he is appointed on the same terms as the Rector. In the draft we have provisions on the rules of appointment and dismissal of the Centre's Vice-Directors, and the appointment of the Director is not subject to any relevant provisions. There is also a lack of provisions to determine what matters will be dealt with in the statutes of the centre. In view of the specific characteristics of the centre, it would appear that at least those areas which differ from public universities should be identified in the project and that the possibility of a different regulation in the CMKP Statute should be eliminated. The question should be asked why the government resigned from setting up the centre as a research institute. According to Art. 1 paragraph 1 The Law on Research Institutes is a State Organizational Unit [[♪ The bell ♪]] isolated... Half a minute, Marshal. ...in legal and organisational terms. The post-graduate medical training centre fulfils all conditions to function as an institute as well. For many years nothing has been done in this regard despite the changing circumstances surrounding the functioning of the post-graduate training centre. The Minister of Health of the current government decided to put this situation in order and began work on a draft law on the Centre for Postgraduate Medical Training, resulting in a specific project which was submitted to the Sejm - and was given the number of the sejm printing - in May this year. This document covered all types of areas that are defined, which are defined in this draft law. As you know very well, in the meantime the Law on Higher Education was being examined, which is the general law to which this project, which we are currently discussing, had to adapt. As you know perfectly well, the Law on Higher Education and Science has entered into force since 1 October, and in fact in terms of financial regulation since 1 January 2019, it was published at the end of August this year. In view of the above, it was necessary to adapt the provisions of this proposal to the new reality which emerged after the review, to complete the legislative process concerning the law which I mentioned. Therefore, in this project, which you are talking about and which you are criticizing, there are terms which result from this printing in the Seym in May. The current project, which you have received, is already fully adapted to the provisions of the new law, which is of a systemic nature. This project, which we are discussing today, is also a draft law of a very general nature, but very important from the point of view of the functioning of the post-graduate training centre. As we all know, the post-graduate education centre is a very important organisation, an entity that deals with education, primarily post-graduate education, doctors, physiotherapists, other health professionals who have graduated from higher education. In changed circumstances, this fixation was very weak and it was therefore decided to change the system, i.e. primarily to give legal personality. The consequences of giving legal personality are issues related to the conduct of the financial economy. So, at this point in time, the Centre may have obtained funds from various sources, from which it has so far not been able to use any other legal form. The Minister of Health has institutes that are targeted strictly at scientific activities, while due to the specific and fundamental tasks of the Postgraduate Medical Training Centre, we considered that this character is the most appropriate. Mrs April asked about the possibility of obtaining grants. I have already presented this information in the first part of my reply. ParlaMint-PL_2018-09-12-sejm-68-1.u139	Thank you, sir. The discussion is closed.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-09-12-sejm-68-1.u140	Position of the club PSL - ED in relation to the government draft law on Postgraduate Medical Training Centre, prints No. 2548 and 2822. The Act will enable you to obtain the title of specialist in the areas applicable to health care. The title of a specialist will be obtained after completion of specialization training or after recognition of the existing scientific or professional achievements as equivalent to the implementation of the specialization programme and the sentence of the State Specialization Examination in a given field - PESoz. E.g. psychologist after completing the training and sentence of PESoz will be able to become a specialist in clinical psychology, a speechpeda - a specialist in neurologopedia and a medical physicist - a specialist in medical physics. The preparation of the Act was necessary because the regulation of the Minister of Health of 30 September 2002 on obtaining the title of specialist in the areas applicable to health protection expired on 2 July 2016. The regulations proposed in the draft law are based on the solutions contained in this Regulation. The Act does not apply to persons engaged in medical professions for which the rules for obtaining the title of specialist are laid down in other legislation, e.g. Doctors and nurses. The draft law lays down the rules for establishing a specialization training programme in the field of health care, the rules for obtaining accreditation for the provision of specialization training in the field of health care. The principles of supervision of the implementation of specialization training, including its control, as well as the conditions and modalities for obtaining the title of specialist in health care, including the holding of specialization training and the organisation and conduct of the State Specialization Examination in the field concerned, are also presented. Specialization training - so far - will take place on the basis of specialization training programmes prepared by a team of experts, appointed by the Director of the CMKP Medical Postgraduate Training Centre - and approved by the Minister of Health. Specialized training in post-graduate education will be provided by training units that have been accredited and included in the list of accredited units led by the Director of the Post-graduate Medical Training Centre. The project also sets out the procedure for accreditation by training bodies. The applicant for accreditation will submit to the Director of the CMKP through the Monitoring System for Medical Workers Training - the application for accreditation in electronic form, signed by a qualified electronic signature or signed by a trusted ePUAP profile. According to the draft law, for the purpose of qualifying designated persons for specialization training, the voivodship will conduct the qualification procedure - from 16 December to 15 January and from 1 June to 30 June of each year - which will include an assessment of compliance with the conditions set out in the law. If it turns out that the number of persons fulfilling these conditions is higher than the number of training places, the competition procedure will be carried out. To date, the qualification procedure has been conducted in training units involving voivodships and voivodship consultants from specific fields. The new solution is to regulate the rules for monitoring the implementation of specialization training provided by the training unit. It will be run by a control team appointed by the Director of the Postgraduate Medical Training Centre. The scope of the inspection activities and the powers of the controllers, as well as the documentation drawn up as a result of the checks and related procedures have also been defined. Impacts were identified for the training unit: in the event of a finding of irregularities during checks and failure to follow-up recommendations within the prescribed time limit, the training unit will lose accreditation to carry out specialisation training and will be deleted from the list of accredited bodies. These solutions will contribute to improving the quality of specialisation training. The draft law sets out detailed rules for the accession and submission of the State Specialization Examination in a given field - PESoz. It has been proposed to change the admission to this examination. On the basis of the previous regulations, the person who specialized in the exam and submitted a specialization card to the voivodship centre, together with the application for admission to the exam, could be admitted to the exam. The voivodship centre, after establishing that the documents fulfilled the formal conditions, passed them to the Centre for Medical Examinations. However, the provisions did not specify how the missing elements of the training programme could be supplemented. The competent entity for verifying the conduct of specialization training will be the voivode. It will act as a national consultant in a particular area of health care or in a related area if there is no national consultant in a given area and request an assessment of the compatibility of the specialized training with the specialization training programme of the person concerned. In the event of a non-fulfilment of the specialisation training programme, the national health consultant or related consultant will indicate the missing scope of the programme. Then the voivode will direct such a person to the appropriate training unit in order to implement the missing elements of the programme and set the deadline for their implementation. The person who has completed specialization training according to his programme - which will be confirmed by the voivode - will be able to apply to PESoz. The National Specialization Exam for every field of health care is organized by the Medical Exam Centre - CEM. PESoz is carried out by the State Examination Board - PKE. The draft law also provides for the extensive use of electronic systems in the process of obtaining the title of specialist in the areas applicable to health protection. First of all, the Monitoring System for Medical Workers Training - SMC is planned to be launched. It will speed up the process of specialisation training and ensure transparency of the procedures used in the training of healthcare professionals in the areas applicable to health care. It will also make it possible to replace the paper circulation of electronic documents. The IT system provides for specialization training systems, among others. the introduction of electronic specialisation cards documenting the course of training, which will replace the existing paper-based specialisation cards. In addition, the Monitoring System for the Training of Medical Workers will be able to apply for accreditation for the provision of specialized training, specialization training in the selected field applicable to health care, as well as accession to PESoz. The bill also provides for the possibility of joining the State Specialization Exam by a person who did not have time to submit it. This means abolishing the existing time limits for taking the exam. PSL Club - ED will support the proposed solutions.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-09-12-sejm-68-1.u141	We will proceed to the third reading of the bill in the voting block. We proceed with the examination of item 6 of the agenda: First reading of the government draft law on employee capital plans (print No 2811). I ask the Undersecretary of State at the Ministry of Finance of Mr Piotr Nowak to present the justification for the bill.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-09-12-sejm-68-1.u142	Dear Marshal, Ladies and gentlemen! I would like to present to you the draft Act on Employee Capital Plans - a comprehensive regulation on the establishment of employee capital plans as a programme of universal, voluntary, long-term savings collection. The draft law implements the assumptions introduced in the framework of the capital building programme, which is a key element adopted by the government in 2017 and the strategy for responsible development. The capital building programme is designed to build long-term savings and increase the financial security of citizens and the sustainability of the public finance system, develop the local capital market and improve the potential of the economy. One of the priorities of the Capital Construction Programme is to build a stable long-term and universal voluntary pension scheme based on third-party austerity programmes. Employee capital plans as one of the pillars of the Capital Construction Programme will be designed to systematically collect long-term savings by their participants for payment after the end of their professional activity. The introduction of universal long-term savings is justified in order to provide citizens with adequate value for their old age and decent living after reaching 60 years of age. The introduction of a uniform age for men and women giving entitlement to the payment of the funds collected is justified, in particular, by the Union's requirements for equal treatment in respect of, inter alia. to voluntary pension insurance schemes. The programme of employee capital plans is intended for about 11.5 million people employed. The first groups of people employed in the largest entities employing at least 250 people will be able to start collecting savings under the PPK from 1 July 2019. The programme will then be extended every 6 months to further groups of actors, including public finance units. The reasonableness of the gradual introduction of the PPK results from the scale of the project and the need to prepare the system infrastructure on the part of the Polish Development Fund as an institution performing the organisational and coordination function of the project, the preparation of financial institutions for the management of the PPK funds, as well as the proper preparation of the employment entities, in particular in the field of staff systems. High Izbo! I would like to stress that the draft law clearly justifies the fact that the funds collected under the PPK will constitute private ownership of the participants. The PPK's introduced saving mechanism will be an important instrument for collecting the individual participants' own savings in order to use them after they have completed their professional activities. The proposed solutions provide for the voluntary collection of savings under this programme. Each person employed will be automatically enrolled in the program and will be able to withdraw from making contributions to the PPK on the basis of a written declaration submitted to the employer. A return to the program was assumed every 4 years. The employer will inform the participant who has resigned from making payments, re-payments and resume payments unless the participant re-subscribes from the payment on the basis of an appropriate declaration made at the workplace. At the same time, the PPK participant who has resigned from making payments will be able to submit a request to the employer at any time to re-establish them and thus return to the programme. Payments will be made by the employer and the PPK participant from their own resources. The basic contribution financed by the employer will be 1,5% the remuneration on which the pension contributions are based. On the other hand, the basic contribution financed by the participant will in principle be 2%. remuneration, with the exception of less earners who will be able to make a basic payment of not less than 0,5% salaries. In addition, the employer will be able to declare an additional payment of 2,5% in the PPK management agreement. In turn, the participant will be able to declare an additional payment of up to 2% salaries. As a result, a total deposit of 3,5 and8% may be made to the participant's account. In order to increase the incentive to raise savings under these programmes, the proposed law also provides for special support for participants from the State financed by the Labour Fund. The incentive to start and continue saving is to be a one-time so-called one-off. a welcome deposit of PLN 250 per participant. The annual annual payment of PLN 240 will also be encouraged to make systematic savings. Investment funds or pension funds in which funds will be collected under the PPK will locate the assets collected in accordance with the interests of the participants, while maintaining the safety and efficiency of making investments and reducing investment risk. The funds to be managed will be placed in the so-called. funds of a defined date corresponding to at least the number of restrictions on the level of investment risk according to the age of the participant. They will apply different investment policy principles, taking into account the different age of participants, ensuring increasing security as the participant approaches 60 years of age. It must be stressed that the remuneration of individual financial institutions for the management of defined-date funds cannot be greater than 0,5% the value of net assets per year. In addition, financial institutions will be entitled to pay a maximum of 0,1% for the result achieved. the net asset value of the fund manager on a yearly basis, provided that the positive return rate is not lower than the reference rate. The supervision of the functioning of the capital programmes will be exercised by the Financial Supervision Commission as regards compliance with the right to conduct the PPK and compliance with the interests of the participants in those programmes. They will be entitled to all supervisory powers set out in the sectoral laws applicable to individual financial institutions. Where a financial institution or the fund has found an infringement of a defined date, the provisions of the proposed law relating to the functioning of the programme of the Financial Supervision Commission may apply the sanctions laid down in the relevant sectoral laws. An important element of the new savings system will be the records of employees' capital programmes kept by the Polish Development Fund in the ICT system. In order to ensure an adequate level of security of the PPK system from the perspective of the participants in the proposed Act, the requirement to allow the financial institution to register the obligation to meet the relevant experience in the management of investment funds or pension funds, capital requirements as well as investment policy requirements is set out in the proposed Act. The inclusion of the financial institution in the records of the PPK will be possible after the submission of a declaration to the PFR and the KNF of the fulfilment of the conditions in question and the transmission of documents confirming their fulfilment. By default, i.e. without having to comply with them, a designated financial institution, i.e. will be included in the PPK's records. TFI in which more than 50% the share capital is owned by the PFR. A portal for staff capital programmes will also be set up for the presentation of offers from financial institutions addressed to employment entities, for the presentation of information on the requirements for employment entities, the rights and obligations of persons employed, and for the future also to inform participants of the value of the funds they have collected. It will be run by the portal operator, i.e. by a subsidiary of PFR. In view of the specific savings targets collected under these schemes, they will in principle remain on the PPK participant's account until the payment, transfer or return is made. The basic principle will be the possibility of paying them once the participant reaches 60 years of age. Then 25% the funds collected will be paid to the participant once, unless he applies for payment of that part of the funds in instalments. Other 75% the funds collected will be paid in at least 120 monthly instalments, giving a period of 10 years. In addition, in the event of a serious illness of the participant, the participant's spouse or child may at any time apply for payment of up to 25% of the funds. In such cases, payment, depending on the participant's request, will either be made once or in instalments and, importantly, will not entail a repayment obligation. The PPK participant will also be able to pay up to 100% once the value of the funds collected in his account to cover his own contribution in connection with borrowing to finance the construction of a residential building, the acquisition of an apartment or land property, with the obligation to repay those funds within 15 years from the date of their payment. The draft law is intended to enter into force as a general rule on 1 January 2019. In view of the above, I ask the High House to work constructively and urgently on the draft Act on Employee Capital Plans submitted. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2018-09-12-sejm-68-1.u195	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Freedom and Solidarnia Circle, I present my position on the Government's draft Act on Employee Capital Plans (Print No 2811). The need for changes is due to the need to implement the strategy adopted by the Council of Ministers for responsible development. One of the important instruments for implementing the SOR is the Capital Building Programme (PBK) consisting in building savings for Poles. PBK performs strategic functions in the field of economic reconstruction of the country and increases the financial security of citizens and stability of the public finance system. In addition, the implementation of the programme will have a positive impact on the development of the local capital market and will increase the investment potential of the country. The contested law concerns the implementation of an effective and voluntary austerity scheme complementing the current pension system. Through the introduction of employee capital plans (PPK) outlined in the SOR, the Capital Construction Programme will have functional opportunities to create investment policies conducive to the development of the local capital market, re-burerocratise pension schemes and provide easy access to information about programmes for participants. At the same time, building a stable long-term, universal and voluntary pension system based on the third pillar savings schemes in the business sector will increase the wealth and financial security of Poles during the retirement period. In addition, the developed capital market is an essential element for building a strong basis for economic development, which provides an opportunity to increase individual income and thus fosters GDP growth. There is also a proportionate relationship between the country's large economic potential and a stable pension system with high pension benefits. The proposed bill will promote this direction of desired change. The argument behind the introduction of PPK is the fact that 3/4 Poles do not save money for old age. Therefore, the problem of not saving for pension purposes is important in relation to the increasing gap between remuneration and the projected amount of pension benefits from the general pension security system. In order to combat poverty and to maintain the real value of pension benefits, it is therefore necessary to ensure diversification of sources of income financing during the retirement period. The awareness of demographic processes also requires a careful look at the financial situation of the Social Insurance Fund. I will remind you here the forecast of EUROPOP 2013. It says that in 2060, compared to 2015, the total population will decrease by 5.4 million people (13.9% decrease). ), the population of pre-production age will decrease by 1.7 million people (a decrease of 24.8% ), the working age population will decrease by 8.0 million people (down 33.3%) After-production population will increase by 4.4 million people (an increase of 58.0% ). In order to stabilise the general pension system and maintain the current level of protection of life needs after retirement age, efforts should be made to increase labour productivity by improving the organisation and efficiency of the economy, implementing innovative technologies within Industry 4.0 and providing additional savings in PPK, as well as developing technologically advanced financial markets. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! The Free and Solidarni Circle will support the government's draft law on employee capital plans.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-09-12-sejm-68-1.u196	A request was made in the discussion to reject the draft law at first reading. We will proceed to the vote on this proposal in the voting block. ParlaMint-PL_2018-09-12-sejm-68-1.u197	Dear Marshal, High Izbo! I am pleased to present a government bill amending the Personal Income Tax Act and some other laws, print no. 2809. This is a project, you can say, revolutionary. It does one very important thing: photos of individuals' income tax taxpayers' obligation to make tax returns on their own. Thus, there is a significant simplification of the functioning of tax law. From now on, any person who wishes to benefit from the procedure set out in this draft will be able, you can say, ultimately not submit a PIT, to use the mechanism, the procedure which is presented, designed in the act in such a way that the tax office will fill out an annual statement for the taxpayer and thus the taxpayer will no longer have to do anything. Thus, it is really important to improve the functioning of the tax administration system, the personal income tax system. This is important - I would like to stress - that this does not mean that other ways of making tax returns will cease to exist. Any person who wishes to make a correction, fill in a paper form, use all existing means of submitting an annual statement electronically will still be able to do so. There is, you can say, the possibility of extending this by the additional possibility of not submitting a tax return. The project will, of course, also reduce the cost of collecting personal income tax. As we know, the greatest cost for the tax administration is the need to save electronic statements in a paper version. So we're assuming that some of the statements that are being filed in paper today will no longer be filed. As I said, the project assumes that these statements will automatically be filled out by the tax authorities, thanks to the fact that the tax administration will have data on taxpayers. These figures are, of course, based on statements submitted by employers by the Social Insurance Institution on income for the previous year. Tax returns will be prepared for all taxpayers. Anyone will be able to decide if they want to use it. They will be available from 15 February to 30 April of the following year after tax year and KAS, i.e. the National Tax Administration, will provide tax returns to taxpayers, based on information from payers, data provided by payers in the last year's statement, i.e. a child relief, an entry for a public benefit organisation, which they gave 1% last year. , and the data contained in the records of the head of KAS, such as the amount of advance payments for personal income tax paid during the tax year, and other registers, e.g. ZUS register for paid contributions or PESEL - for children's benefits. So, in fact, this will allow the taxpayer not to give an annual statement in any way, and the tax office, based on the information available, will fill it out in an optimal way for the citizen. There is one important thing - it is worth remembering - that this possibility is offered, and will naturally concern them, the clearing taxpayers individually, together with the spouse, in a way also intended for single parents. The tax return prepared by KAS will be made available to taxpayers in electronic form in the application on the tax portal. This portal is a standard procedure for filling in a yearly statement, i.e., it can be said that before automatic confirmation on the last day on which the statement is due, the taxpayer will be able to correct it, it will be able to make changes there, for example, due to the fact that it wants to make changes in the reliefs or to change the organisation it wants to transfer 1%. The tax return prepared by KAS will be verifiable, accepted without change. In case of PIT-37 statements, PIT-38 doesn't have to do anything. Then, at the end of the deadline, this statement will be automatically accepted. They can be modified or rejected and settled on their own, thanks to this whole range of ways that are available. It will also, of course, be possible to verify and update the account number reported to the tax office for the refund of the overpayment. In the case of settlements for 2018, the statements will be available to taxpayers, which is what we are really talking about, which should already work in the context of settlements for 2018, i.e. at the beginning of 2019. In the beginning, what we are proposing will be offered for the reasons that, so to speak, IT non-business natural persons making a statement on the PIT-37 form, and taxpayers obtaining income from cash capital, proceeds from the sale of shares, making a statement on the PIT-38 form. And in the following year, when we settle for 2019, it will of course be at the beginning of 2020, also tax returns will be able to be submitted by individuals conducting non-agricultural economic activities, making a statement on forms PIT-36L and PIT-28. So in fact, in the first year, this system will be available mainly to non-business individuals, but in the following year this functionality will be available more widely. The project provides for a reduction of the deadline for repayment of the excess payment resulting from the submission of the taxpayer's statement of income tax on individuals from 3 months to 45 days, provided that the above. the statement shall be made by electronic means. There is, you can say, a certain incentive that if we give a statement in an electronic version, this overpayment will be refunded faster. This is also important for the efficiency of the tax administration. The draft also introduces provisions aimed at protecting the taxpayer if the taxpayer accepts a misconception prepared by the tax authority. Then, of course, there is no criminal interest. The fault of the body removes liability from the taxpayer. A mechanism has been put in place to effectively inform the taxpayer of the tax return to be paid. We also had a discussion at the consultation stage, which will happen if the taxpayer wants to use this method of lodging, so, let's say, he won't do anything. It will turn out that he will have to pay some small tax resulting from the fact that the advances do not cover the sum of the tax liability, will pass the deadline for the tax return at the end of April and therefore there will be a question of what if the taxpayer did not see, looked, did not care, used this method of tax return, so he does not even know how much tax to pay. And here we have introduced a mechanism that assumes that the tax office has a duty to inform the taxpayer of the amount of the tax liability. We will use all available methods, i.e. If the taxpayer leaves a phone number, an e-mail, then of course we will use this channel to provide information. If not, it will be information in the letter version, i.e. communication in a traditional way. Introduction the solutions relate primarily to a change in the time limit for the submission of tax returns, i.e. between 15 February and the end of April, by shortening the deadline for the transmission of information by taxpayers to the tax office chief - all payers will be required to send information to the tax office chief by the end of January of the year following the tax year, so that this mechanism can act as soon as possible - the obligation for taxable persons to transmit information by electronic means only - thus resolving the problem of annual testimony will be applicable - and changing the way in which advances are levied on personal income tax. This change concerns the amount of advances collected during the month in which the taxable person's income from the beginning of the year at the workplace in question exceeded the upper limit of the range, i.e. the transition between the first and second thresholds. According to the project, during the month of overrun, the advance will be charged at two rates of 18% and 32%. This is an improvement, of course, directly related to this project. In general, this is sometimes the case for a small proportion of taxpayers, which is covered by the second scale, that the current legal provisions do not exactly specify, so to speak, how much is there in the month of transition to payment of the advance. And so that there is no discrepancy between the final amount of tax to be paid and advances, here we are introducing this small modification so that the amounts paid after the end of the year are as close as possible to those amounts fixed, the sum of the advances collected during the tax year. So thank you very much and encourage you to support the project. Thank you. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2018-09-12-sejm-68-1.u199	Thank you very much. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! The Law and Justice Club supports the changes proposed by the government in the personal income tax collection scheme proposed in print No 2809 and proposes that this project be transferred to the Public Finance Commission for further work. Ladies and gentlemen, as a result of the proposed amendments, the costs of the tax system will be reduced and the proposed and announced in the Law and Justice Programme will be supported by taxpayers in the submission of tax returns on personal income tax by the National Tax Administration. The tax return prepared by the National Tax Administration will be available to all tax payers individually, together with their spouses, and in the way provided for single parents. When making a statement, the National Tax Administration will use a database of information on the taxpayer, created on the basis of inter alia. information received from payers, from last year's PIT statement, which contains information on whether the taxable person benefits from child relief for which public benefit organisation the taxable person has transferred 1% the tax due, and the information received from the Social Security Office in respect of the social and health contributions paid. The taxpayer will have an insight into the statement prepared by the National Tax Administration. He will be able to check his tax return and, in the absence of comments, accept it. It will be able to update the data and supplement them with those which the National Tax Administration did not have, e.g. the data arising from the fact that the taxable person is engaged in a business activity or made in a tax year a deduction of the donation. Where the taxpayer has no comments on the statement drawn up by the National Tax Administration, this statement shall be accepted automatically on expiry of the time limit for the submission of the annual statement. The taxpayer may also reject the statement prepared by the National Tax Administration and account for himself. Therefore, the form of the tax return is ultimately decided by the taxpayer himself. The National Tax Administration will provide a prepared tax return from 15 February to 30 April of the year following the tax year. Taxpayers will have unlimited access to these statements and to official confirmation of receipt of the statement to the tax authority. These taxpayers won't have to do anything but check the data. Also 25% clearing taxpayers who are jointly with their spouses and who do not benefit from any reductions and deductions will be able to benefit from this scheme. The government leaves the taxpayer the opportunity to testify in the past form, namely paper or electronic form.
