I ask the Undersecretary of State at the Ministry of Finance of Mr Piotr Nowak to present the justification for the project.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-09-12-sejm-68-1.u215	Dear Marshal! High Izbo! The proposed law amending certain laws in connection with the strengthening of supervision and investor protection in the financial market aims to strengthen the coordination of state policy against developments in the financial market, including the early identification of risks related to its functioning. The project introduces three major changes concerning the supervision of the financial market, financial education of the public and the issuance of certain securities. Firstly, in order to strengthen the supervision of the financial market, the project proposes changes to the organisation and functioning of the Authority of the Financial Supervision Commission, which will become a state legal entity once the proposed law enters into force. This will increase the financial independence of this office and thus strengthen the supervision of the financial market. UKNF will be able to manage its resources more efficiently and allocate them to statutory tasks such as increasing the frequency of inspections in supervised entities, analysis of reporting data and cooperation with financial market supervisors in other countries. At the same time, it should be stressed that this does not mean full freedom on the part of the authorities to spend these funds, as the budget of the NCF will be approved by the President of the Council of Ministers and sent together with the draft budget law as an annex to the Sejm. In addition, the Act extends the membership of the Financial Supervision Committee by four additional members, including representatives of the President of the Council of Ministers, the Banking Guarantee Fund, the President of the Office for Competition and Consumer Protection and the Minister - a member of the Council of Ministers responsible for coordinating the activities of special services. Additional members of the committee, with the exception of the representative of the President of the Council of Ministers, will not take part in the votes because they will only be members with an advisory vote. The extension of the composition of the Financial Supervision Commission will ensure better cooperation between State authorities in the supervision of the proper functioning of the financial market. The provisions of the proposed law also allow for mutual exchange of information, including by law, between the Financial Supervision Commission and the Internal Security Agency, the Central Anti-corruption Office, the National Tax Administration, the Police and the President of the Office for Competition and Consumer Protection, which in turn will allow the identification of possible risks of irregularities that may occur in the financial market. The exchange of information will also allow for coordination of the abovementioned activities. bodies vis-à-vis entities that pose a real threat to financial market participants. In the field of financial education, the bill envisages the creation of a Financial Education Fund, whose main task will be to raise the financial awareness of Poles, including the development of knowledge about investment products offered. In particular, the Fund will develop financial education strategies, organise and support educational projects, organise information campaigns. The Fund will also cooperate with the Ministry of National Education. The proposed legislation will also create a Financial Education Council, which will include, among others, representatives of the Minister responsible for Financial Institutions, the Minister responsible for Education and Education, the President of the National Bank of Poland, the President of the Office for Competition and Consumer Protection and the Financial Ombudsman. The Council will be responsible for determining the direction and supervision of the Fund's activities. The source of the financial education fund will be the funds derived from the administrative penalties imposed on financial institutions by the Financial Supervision Authority and the Financial Ombudsman, as well as certain administrative penalties imposed by the President of the OOKiK and the Audit Supervision Committee, as well as the financial statements and benefits decided by the court. The latest major changes envisaged by the project are the introduction of mandatory dematerialisation of corporate bonds, investment certificates issued by closed investment funds and pledge letters. These financial instruments will be subject to mandatory registration in the CSD's securities deposit under the rules currently in force for public securities and government bonds. The creation of a single central repository of information on the amount of debt issued by individual issuers and the timeliness of its repayment will increase the transparency of the securities issued in Poland, as it will facilitate the access to such information by the Financial Supervision Commission and other competent state authorities, as well as interested investors. The proposed law is to enter into force 14 days after the date of publication, with the exception of the provisions relating to the financing and organisation of committees, as well as the dematerialisation of securities which will enter into force on 1 January 2019. High Izbo! By asking for the support of the bill submitted, I would like to express my conviction on behalf of the government that its adoption will contribute to improving the supervision of the national financial market and, at the same time, strengthen the protection of the interests of individual investors operating in this area. Thank you very much.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-09-12-sejm-68-1.u216	Thank you very much. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear five minutes' statements on behalf of clubs and circles. I'm opening a discussion. I would ask Mr Grzegorz to speak on behalf of the Law and Justice Club... excuse me, Kazimierz Smolinski.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-09-12-sejm-68-1.u217	Mrs. Marshal! High Seym! Minister! I have the honour to present the position of the Law and Justice Club on the draft law amending certain laws in connection with the strengthening of supervision and investor protection in the financial market, print No 2812. The purpose of the proposed regulation is to change the financing method of the Financial Supervision Authority and the Financial Supervision Authority to provide funding directly from fees paid by supervised financial market entities. The project also aims to ensure better coordination of state policy towards the financial market and early identification of risks related to its functioning, thereby guaranteeing the security of investors, which is to be achieved by extending the composition of the Financial Supervision Commission. The members of the committee will be a representative of the President of the Council of Ministers, a representative of the Banking Guarantee Fund, a representative of the President of the Office for Competition and Consumer Protection and a representative of the Minister - a member of the Council of Ministers responsible for coordinating the activities of special services. The last three members will only have an advisory vote. The proposed law also establishes a legal framework for the provision of information between KNF and its members and other bodies such as ABW, CBA, KAS, Police and the UOKiK. In addition, the provisions of the Act allow for the exchange of information obtained in connection with the work of the Commission by institutions with their representatives. KNF and the National Bank of Poland will be able to exchange information obtained in the course of their tasks on a reciprocal basis. The proposed solution will also apply to the exchange of information between the KNF and the Bank Guarantee Fund and the Minister responsible for financial institutions, as well as to the transmission of information to the European Central Bank. The membership of the representative of the President of the Council of Ministers in committee is crucial in the context of the planning and implementation of State policy in relation to the financial market and at the same time ensures proper coordination between the various ministers in this area. The bill also provides for the creation of a Financial Education Fund at the National Securities Deposit. The aim of the fund will be to increase the financial awareness of Poles. Its main task will be to promote knowledge of financial markets. The Financial Education Board, consisting of representatives of state institutions having an impact on the development and security of the financial market and education, will be responsible for overseeing the fund and determining its direction. The source of the fund will be financial resources from, inter alia, the administrative penalties imposed by the Commission for Financial Supervision, certain administrative penalties imposed by the President of the Office for Competition and Consumer Protection, the Audit Supervision Commission and the Financial Ombudsman, as well as the formations and cash benefits decided by the courts for a national deposit, as well as the funds which have been forfeit by the court. For 2019, the penalty amount imposed by the Financial Supervision Commission is planned to amount to PLN 9 million. The next objective of the proposed amendment is to introduce mandatory dematerialisation of corporate bonds, investment certificates issued by closed investment funds and pledge letters regardless of whether they were the subject of a public offer and whether they are intended to be traded on any trading venue. Another objective is to define a legal framework for the trading of debt instruments in a dematerialised way to ensure better investor protection and more effective supervision of debt instruments put on the market. More security and flexibility for investors are also in favour of the proposed change. The registration system operated by the CSD is the safest system, and registration in this system of all non-public securities will lead to the registration of only the best-prepared entities for this task, i.e. brokerage houses and banks authorised to hold securities accounts and participants in the National Securities Deposit, and not, as yet, entities that are not entitled to hold securities accounts. Currently, both the KNF and the Financial Supervision Authority are financed through the State budget entirely by contributions and charges paid to supervised entities, which means that expenditure is neutral for the budget deficit. Once the Authority of the Financial Supervision Commission has been transformed into a State legal person, contributions and fees will be paid directly to that office where the Financial Supervision Authority will be the management body. The extension of the membership of the KNF will ensure better cooperation between state institutions. Financial instruments will be subject to mandatory registration, which will increase the transparency of debt securities issues. In view of the above arguments, the Law and Justice Club supports the draft law and will vote in favour of passing it to [[♪ The bell ♪]] work in the Committee on Public Finance. Thank you very much.

That's why there's dematerialization, so there's a central register where it's clear. This will increase the transparency of turnover. So the introduction of dematerialisation is also welcomed by all de facto members or stakeholders of this process. ParlaMint-PL_2018-09-12-sejm-68-1.u243	Mr. Marshal! High Seym! I would like to present the position of the Members' Circle of Free and Solidarnia in relation to the report of the Committee on Maritime Affairs and Inland Navigation, the Committee on Social Policy and Family Affairs and the Committee on Foreign Affairs on the Government's draft Law on the ratification of the Amendment to the Maritime Labour Convention of 2006, adopted by the International Labour Conference in Geneva on 9 June 2016. The conditions of employment of seafarers on ships, i.e. health care, medical care or social and social security, are very important acts of international law which regulate the working conditions of seafarers in the waters of the world. At present, the rules on sailors flying Polish flags are not equal to those of sailors who work under foreign flags. The proposed amendments aim to improve the factual situation regarding legal or health assistance in the event of ship-based intimidation or harassment. This has a huge impact on the quality of work and, above all, the mental state of the sailor. Rejection of this case may lead to an increase in the incidence of seafarers, which will translate into the quality of the work done and a decrease in the efficiency of companies and the maritime industry as a whole. Single cases can affect the entire crew, so elimination of such omissions is important. Therefore, ratification of these amendments is a necessity, and as the Circle of Members of the Free and Solidarnia, we are in favour of making amendments.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-09-12-sejm-68-1.u244	Dear Marshal, High Izbo! On behalf of the Kukiz Club15, I have the honour to present my position on the Government's draft law on ratification of the Amendment to the Maritime Labour Convention of 2006 adopted by the International Labour Conference in Geneva on 9 June 2016, print No 2788. This bill regulates the conditions of employment of seafarers, life on board, health and medical care, social security and social protection of seafarers. It is necessary to carry out the ratification procedure in this case, given that it is an essential act guaranteeing and protecting the rights of crew members of maritime vessels. One of the most important issues of the project is the provisions on workplace accidents and injuries and occupational diseases aimed at eliminating harassment and intimidation on board the ship. We are aware that intimidation and harassment in the workplace can have serious consequences for the physical and emotional health of workers, resulting in reduced motivation, increased absenteeism and reduced productivity. However, they can also have negative effects on companies themselves, resulting in a deterioration of working conditions and serious consequences. The implementation of the amendments will certainly contribute to the prevention of such threats on ships. In addition, the project provides for e.g. the obligation to guarantee the rights of seafarers working on Polish ships on an equal footing with seafarers working on foreign ships, which will consequently improve the conditions of employment of Polish shipowners. Amendments to the Code implementing Regulation 5.1 and Annex A5-II to the MLC will simplify the procedure for obtaining the Maritime Labour Certificate by allowing the renewal of the certificate for a further period not exceeding five years when, after completion of the inspection carried out for its renewal, it appears that the vessel still complies with the requirements of national law or other legislation to comply with the requirements of the Convention in question and the new certificate cannot be issued immediately and made available on board the vessel concerned. The aim of this project is to regulate and organise the existing EU legal framework on the safety and conditions of persons employed on ships, so as to ensure the proper application of EU legislation, and Kukiz15 supports the proposed project.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-09-12-sejm-68-1.u245	High Seym! Mr. Marshal! On behalf of the Club of the Polish People's Party - Union of European Democrats, I will present my position on the Commission's report on the Government's bill on the ratification of the Amendment to the Maritime Labour Convention of 2006, adopted by the International Labour Conference in Geneva on 9 June 2016. By regulating the conditions for hiring seafarers and life on board, health and medical care, social security and social protection of seafarers, the Convention is an important act of international law guaranteeing and protecting the rights of crew members of maritime vessels. Given the obligation to guarantee the rights of seafarers working on Polish ships on an equal footing with seafarers employed on foreign ships, ratification of the 2016 amendments is essential. To adopt the Amendment to the Code implementing Regulation 4.3 in part: Guideline B4. 3.1 - The provisions on accidents at work and injuries and occupational diseases were the elimination of harassment and intimidation on board, in line with the latest version of the Guidelines on this issue, issued jointly by the International Maritime Transport Chamber and the International Transport Workers' Federation, which should be taken into account in the application of this amendment. Furthermore, it was recognised that harassment and intimidation on board could have serious consequences for the physical and emotional health of seafarers and lead to a weakening of their motivation at work and to an increase in disease. The Law on the ratification of the Amendment to the Maritime Labour Convention of 2006, adopted by the International Labour Conference in Geneva on 9 June 2016, deserves the support of and the Modern Support of this Act will give. Article 89 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 I Constitution, i.e. with the prior consent expressed in the Act, which is being carried out. Three amendments are the subject of the current ratification. The first concerns the fight against harassment and intimidation on ships. The second and third concern the introduction of a special procedure for extending the validity of a maritime work certificate when the existing one expired and the new certificate cannot be issued immediately, especially when the ship is on a voyage. ParlaMint-PL_2018-09-12-sejm-68-1.u247	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Ladies and gentlemen! I have the honour on behalf of the Law and Justice Club to present my position on the report of the Committee on Maritime Affairs and Navigation, the Committee on Social Policy and Family Affairs and the Committee on Foreign Affairs on the Government's draft Law on the ratification of the Amendment to the Maritime Labour Convention of 2006, adopted by the International Labour Conference in Geneva. The Special Tripartite Committee, set up under Article XIII of the International Labour Organisation Convention on Maritime Labour, adopted amendments to that Convention in 2006, which were approved by the International Labour Conference on 9 June 2016. The International Labour Organisation Convention on Maritime Labour entered into force on 20 August 2013, after its prior ratification by Poland in December 2011. The Convention, as an important act of international law, guarantees and protects the rights of crews of maritime vessels. Its regulations concern the living conditions and employment of seafarers, medical care and health care. It also regulates issues related to social protection and social protection. The ratification of amendments to the Convention aims to ensure the rights of seafarers working on Polish ships to the same extent as those employed on foreign ships. The purpose of the amendments is to: - the elimination of harassment and intimidation on board as phenomena causing serious effects on the physical and emotional health of seafarers, - enable the renewal of the validity of the maritime work certificate, for a maximum period of 5 years, where the vessel continues to comply with the requirements of local law or other requirements of the Convention in question and the new certificate cannot be issued within a short period of time and is available on board the vessel concerned. The entry into force of the 2016 amendments will entail the need to amend the Maritime Labour Act. Amendments related to the prevention of harassment and intimidation in the workplace do not require a change of Polish legislation, as they are already included in the legislation. Ratification of amendments is part of the government's maritime policy. Moreover, it will not increase the State's financial expenditure or reduce revenue. This also applies to local government units. The proposal to ratify the amendments to the Maritime Labour Convention has been consulted with trade unions and employers' organisations. The draft law was also addressed to the Social Dialogue Council. By acting on behalf of the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice, I declare that the club will vote in favour of the bill submitted. Thank you for your attention.

ParlaMint-PL_2018-09-12-sejm-68-1.u257	Thank you, Marshal. Let me speak from here. Mr. President was kind enough to start by referring to the constitution, and so will I. In the light of the provisions of the Constitution, the Supreme Chamber of Control shall be subject to the Sejm. The Law on the Supreme Chamber of Control repeats this fundamental relationship between the Sejm and the only state control institution in Poland. In other words, the Sejm is the only body of our state overseeing the work of NIK. The instruments of this supervision, you can say, are quite ascetic. The main supervisory instrument of the Sejm in the light of the generally applicable law is the annual examination of the report on the activities of the Chamber and its acceptance or non-acceptance. It should be made clear that failure to accept the report does not have any legal or material effects. It is simply a reminder of the House or, to put it practically, a reminder of the House's headship. The Sejm in gremio is not allowed to supervise the House. It shall perform this function through the Parliamentary Committee on State Control. It is the responsibility of following not only the results of the work of the Chamber, which are above all the reports of the NIK, but also its condition and organizational condition. It was precisely the critical assessment of the organisation of the Chamber and the relationship within this institution that led to the recommendation not to adopt the report two years ago and a year ago, as well as today. The Commission for State control has taken the view that the external effects of the work of NIK and its internal situation are being assessed. Having seen errors or negligence in the internal organisation of the Chamber and using the only disciplinary measure it has at its disposal, namely the possibility of rejecting the report, it has previously requested that the report not be adopted. That's the way it's supposed to be this time. Over the years, the work of controllers has been built up with formalism. The current NIK authorities accept the absurdity of this situation. This bureaucratic formalism was enchanted in the form of the so-called. a quality assurance system. The report only mentions this system at a dose, but it is described in a document of 88 pages, which accounts for almost 20%. over 500-page report. It follows from this document that, within each planned subject of the audit, as well as in the context of unplanned topics, the involved workers at different stages must fill out 11 types of forms. For example, in the so-called. a summary of the comments submitted to the project, the coordinator of the audit subject, and therefore the key person to achieve the quality of the audit, must specify the comments submitted, indicate whether they have been taken into account, not included or partially taken into account, and describe how they have been taken into account or justified by their failure to take them into account. Most often the comments of a number of reviewers - and this is apparent from the speech of the President - are several dozen, and sometimes several hundred, so the poor coordinator must work through this litany, although none for him. At most, this formalism is made more convenient for reviewers. However, the convenience should be ensured for controllers and coordinators, not reviewers. The whole quality assurance system and other elements of the bureaucracy of the controller's work are simply unnecessary. Please approach this practically. Such mechanisms for recording and documenting work and job assessments make sense in large groups of people, in corporations where thousands of people work. There are about 1,100 controllers working at NIK. In such a few bodies more will be achieved through personal contact, trusting in the common sense of controllers and promoting creativity than through formalized and impersonal procedures. Blind formalization of the work of the Supreme Audit Chamber confirms the current implementation of the model of professional competence of controllers - that was a quote - which is also mentioned in the report only in a few days. Professional competence is to be defined, defined and marked with behavioral indicators and scales that illustrate the different levels of their mastery. The competence profiles assigned to the functions have already been developed, which are to specify precisely what competences are most important. All this was invented behind the desks of the Department of Control and Professional Development Methodology. All this mitrage is currently being tested on the living body of the controllers. However, any paper model of competence will not change this simple fact that in the basic internal structures of NIK, with fewer than 100 people, everyone knows the value of everyone. Without this knowledge, advisers and directors in delegations and control departments could not carry out the tasks assigned to them as effectively as possible. NO-ONE is such a big institution as to stand before the compulsion to introduce complex formalistics, because without it, its leadership is not able to effectively manage the company. Paper formalistics dehumanizes NIK, which contradicts the need to build creative and sacrificial teams. Most of the control staff today are lawyers, administrators and economists. This is because for years there has been a directional selection, seemingly right. Working in state control simply requires intelligence, ability to think logically, analytical ability, creativity, as well as life experience and honesty. By preferring the chosen type of education, NIK may be cut off from potentially interesting candidates for work. If Poland is to develop in the long term as it is now, more and more important will be the issue of a broadly understood engineering character, which lawyers or administrators may not be able to handle. Without a doubt, the strong and sympathetic part of the organisation of work in NIK is that the institution regularly trains, in various ways, its control staff. The report rightly calls these specialized trainings because they are quite narrowly profiled. Supporting this tradition of specialized training, it should be noted that NIK fell into the organizational routine here. Such training is not very attractive for experienced controllers who have worked for many years. Their needs are different. In particular, they are less interested in the control instrument which they have mastered long ago and, more importantly, the substantive side of complex relationships taking place in the sectors they are engaged in. NO ONE has created this type of training offer for experienced controllers and, if I am not mistaken, is not thinking about it. We have repeatedly talked about the fact that the Supreme Audit Chamber uses a method popular in public entities to create specific financial reserves by planning excess amounts of certain expenditures. The difference between these public units and the Supreme Audit Chamber is that this House is the guardian of public expenditure and can be expected to behave exemplaryly. A few examples. About 700,000 were spent on foreign travel in 2016. PLN, and yet for 2017 almost twice as much expenditure was planned: 1300 thousand. PLN, but the amount of 800,000 was spent. zł, i.e. close to 2016 expenditure. In this way, a reserve of about 500,000 was created. zł. In the same way, this reserve was created, in quotes, for the over-planning of domestic journeys, which amounted to PLN 1.5 million. About 1.5 million PLN was spent on training in 2016, but in 2017 an expenditure of about 2,300 thousand was planned. zł, but spent the same amount as in 2016, or 1500 thousand. So the reserve was 800,000. zł. This reserve, artificially created due to the difference between the amounts planned and really spent, of course, is justified in the report. However, it is difficult to believe in these explanations, since this is the case every year, although of course it is not always in the same expenditure items. As I have said many times, the reports of the Supreme Audit Chamber will be of interest even in the case of banal findings, i.e. findings previously well known to professionals in the industry. This is because NIK controls contain documented material. After such arrangements, i.e. documented, there are always people who are interested, and especially public people and journalists. Every reasonable public institution tries to assess the effects of its work. The House has been using certain standards for years. Among them are so-called. financial or reporting effects of irregularities, divided into five categories, as well as financial benefits, divided into three categories. The second group of meters in which the dominant position is so-called is interesting. the amounts of additional income and revenue and the increase in assets resulting from corrective actions taken after the control of the NIK. The total amount in this group in 2017 amounts to approx. 106 million PLN, i.e. almost 40% the implemented budget of the NIK on the expenditure side. The problem with this measure lies in asking what is the causal link between the amounts indicated and the findings of the NIK. An example of the sensibleness of this question can be the amount of more than 85 million PLN, which is 80% These financial benefits registered in 2017, recorded as the result of the 2013 National Bank of Poland audit. It is true that in 2014. The National Bank of Poland changed its investment policy in gold, resulting from analyses carried out in this institution in connection with the expiry of the financial crisis, which appeared in 2008. Due to changes in market conditions, the National Bank of Poland began to pursue a more aggressive policy in this area, with higher revenues. This, too, was the recommendation of the NIK, otherwise correct, only that - as I said - market considerations decided about this change. A few words about the report itself. It counts 521 pages, without annexes 474 pages. A useful, otherwise synthetic description of the findings made within the scope of the control topics and the effects obtained takes 3/4 volumes. However, the report lacks basic analytical data, as in previous reports. In particular, the lack of data that characterize the management of the Supreme Audit Chamber, e.g. with a professional structure of controllers, a structure of remuneration and reward, a structure of workload, etc. Incidentally, in 2016, the efficiency of the body of the chamber inspection staff was about two checks per controller per year, so really little, about twice as little. The lack of data for 2017 makes it impossible to provide an estimate in this respect. In short, the lack of basic data makes it difficult to assess the organisation of the work of the Chamber and is the result of an informed limitation of the Sejm's control function. As you know, the Katowice Public Prosecutor's Office has filed an indictment and a request for criminal liability of the President of NIK. In this sense, it is important that the fact of poor management of the Chamber, of management without concern for a high level of professionalism is confirmed. In previous years, we have indicated a similar style of management of the Chamber and, as you can see, there is no change in this area. The way in which NIK's internal affairs are managed is routine, in fact soulless, which demobilizes the best controllers, and paradoxically, the less creative it is to ensure the safety of work. In the long term, this must bring down the effectiveness of NIK, even if at the moment it is not visible to the naked eye. Therefore, the Commission for State Control requests that the 2017 report should not be adopted, as we have proposed in the past two years. Marshal, thank you very much.

The aim of this law is to ensure that healthy meat and preparations from healthy pigs - meat and preparations from healthy pigs - can be sold without any restriction. [[The preparations have already been made, meat.]] Why is that? The point is that meat from these pigs, as well as preparations from these pigs, can be sold normally, without any restriction, provided, of course, that they are healthy and tested animals which may be slaughtered in a slaughterhouse and that their meat may be intended for consumption. ParlaMint-PL_2018-09-12-sejm-68-1.u294	Thank you, sir. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear five minutes' statements on behalf of clubs and circles. I'm opening a discussion. Mr Krzysztof Szulowski, Parliamentary Club Law and Justice, will be the first to speak.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-09-12-sejm-68-1.u295	Mrs. Marshal! Gentlemen of Ministers! High Izbo! I am pleased to present the position of the club Law and Justice on the draft law on specific arrangements for the occurrence of African swine fever in the territory of the Republic of Poland, prints no. 2792 and 2815. In September 2016, the Sejm of the Republic of Poland adopted the original draft law, which we are updating, whose main purpose was to enable the development of products of animal origin intended for human consumption, produced from meat of swine free from the virus, i.e. healthy but kept in areas where restrictions have been introduced due to the presence of this virus in Poland. Poland, in accordance with the decisions of the European Union, is required to take specific action in designated areas to combat and prevent the spread of the virus. These recommendations also apply to healthy animals and aim primarily at eliminating the risk of the spread of ASF virus and the emergence of its new outbreaks. It should be pointed out that, in the case of this disease, even sick animals do not pose any risk to humans. All the more so, no risk to human health or life is posed by healthy animals from restricted areas, as well as meat from such animals or products. Meat coming from such areas before processing and placing on the market shall be subjected to detailed testing by veterinarians and shall be specially marked. The pigs free from ASF virus may therefore be slaughtered and the meat obtained from them may be further processed, processed and placed on the market. These conditions were at the heart of the legal changes proposed at that time, which allowed for the elimination of unnecessary barriers to the marketing of processed pig meat from healthy pigs kept in areas where cases of ASF were found. These changes consisted in the non-application of the provisions of the Law of 29 January 2004. The right to procurement for the supply of meat products from purely pig meat coming from agricultural holdings situated in areas covered by orders, prohibitions, restrictions and other control measures. The current amendment has similar objectives. The new proposals concern the extension of the possibilities created by the Act for meat products and the inclusion of meat free from ASF on agricultural holdings located in areas covered by orders, prohibitions or restrictions. The proposed solutions will increase the possibility of selling ASF-free pigs for slaughter from the abovementioned holdings. ASF is a disease on which we will be doomed for a very long time. We need to take advantage of our experience and learn to function with this disease. This law is a step in this direction while ensuring safety and not increasing the risk of spread of the disease and ensuring an appropriate price for pig farmers. With all these arguments in mind, the Law and Justice Club will support the bill. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2018-09-12-sejm-68-1.u301	Dear Marshal! High Izbo! The draft provides that the arrangements provided for in the Law of 5 September 2016 on specific arrangements for the occurrence of African swine fever in the territory of the Republic of Poland will also apply to fresh pig meat, tested and free from African swine fever virus, from farms located in areas where outbreaks of that virus have occurred. The aim of this project is to make it easier for agricultural producers to sell meat which will have to comply with the strict veterinary requirements laid down in the legislation. And like my friend said earlier, it's a relief, but that's all. Modern will, of course, vote in favour of the proposed solutions because farmers are waiting for these solutions. But the frustration of pig farmers grows every day, as witnessed by farmers' protests. Farmers complain about growing bioinsurance requirements, huge disposal costs and dramatically low pig buying prices in the ASF region. It is not only the situation of farmers that is dramatic due to the spread of ASF. Also the location of underfunded Veterinarian Inspection staff, which is getting less and less. Their location is very difficult. The objectives of these actions are set out in this report. Maritime authorities shall also monitor the sea shores and review existing sea shore protection structures. The assessment of the dynamics of the coastline allows for effective planning of actions and the use of optimal methods for protecting the shores of the sea taking into account local circumstances. In most cases, actions were related to the removal of the effects of catastrophic storms that hit the Polish coast in December 2016 and January 2017, and their aim was to protect the urbanised facilities. In 2018, 34.5 million PLN was planned for the implementation of artificial beach power supply, reconstruction and construction of shore reinforcements and monitoring of the seashore. The amount planned for the implementation of the programme is more than the amount from 2017 by 500,000. Moreover, the maritime authorities also use the funds of the Operational Programme in the framework of the financing of shore protection, infrastructure and the environment, in total, for these projects 217 million. The measures taken increase the stability of the seashore and prevent its erosion caused, among others. increased frequency and intensity of storms. Particular attention should be paid to the announcement of the continuation of the Marine Coast Protection Programme and work on the new programme already in 2019. This is particularly necessary in the context of climate change being observed and its impact on the coastal area, as the increase in frequency and storm power leads to an accelerated rate of erosion and, consequently, to the degradation of dunes and cliffs, which are a natural element of the marine shore protection system. This could lead to an increased risk of coastal flooding, which is associated with frequent flooding of lowland areas. The increase in average temperatures and the decrease in ice cover in winter months lead to a decrease in the resistance of the shore to washing. It should also be pointed out here that most of the Polish shore are erosion sections. It is still necessary to continue coordinated actions on the whole Polish coast with the guarantee of adequate resources. This is guaranteed by the continuation of the programme. Therefore, on behalf of the Parliamentary Club, I request that the High Sejm adopt the document presented by the Minister for Maritime Affairs and Inland Navigation, on the implementation in 2017 of the Law on the establishment of a multi-annual programme of the Sea Coast Protection Programme, contained in print No 2440. On behalf of the Kukiz Poselski Club15, I have the honour to present my position on the information of the Minister for Maritime Affairs and Inland Navigation on the implementation of the multiannual programme in 2017. The Maritime Coast Protection Programme and the work schedule for 2018 together with the positions of committees, prints No 2440 and 2561. The programme presented is an implementing document for the government's transport development strategy by 2020 (with a view to 2030) in the field of maritime ports. The document diagnoses the current economic situation of Polish seaports and provides forecasts of freight traffic and passenger traffic of Polish seaports by 2020 and prospects for further changes in these markets by 2030. The main aspects of the expansion of this sector of the economy are the development of port infrastructure and sea-side access infrastructure and its adaptation to changing cargo structures, as well as the integration of ports with other actors in transport chains. Undoubtedly, increasing the share of Polish ports in the Baltic Sea basin market is a priority for the development of the economy, given that they form specific poles of development not only regional but also national ones. The proposed investment plans give hope for the competitiveness of Polish ports in the international sense. Among the most important investments included in the programme are among others.: construction of a waterway connecting Vistula Bay with Gdansk Bay, modernization of the Świnoujście - Szczecin water track to a depth of 12.5 m, construction of a central port in Gdansk, construction of an external port in Gdynia and construction of a container terminal in Świnoujście. The implementation of these projects is the basis for the competitiveness of Polish seaports and shipping on a global scale. Currently there are 28 ports in Poland, as many as 19 of which are considered local. Improving the functioning and further development of ports not of fundamental importance for the national economy is the deepening of the watercourse in Świnoujście and the excavation of the Vistula mine. This will improve the port situation in Świnoujście and Elbląg. Swinoujście watercourse - Szczecin connects the port in Szczecin with the sea. The distance of the port 67 km from the Baltic Sea makes it necessary to maintain the parameters of the waterway for sea vessels in the section Lower Odra - Zalew Szczeciński - the outlet of Świny. Swinoujście Track - Szczecin allows you to wrap ships with maximum immersion of up to 9,15 m. The planned deepening of the track will increase the maximum draught of ships to approx. 11 m. As far as the excavation of the Vistula mine is concerned, there is no doubt that it is a key investment, which is of great importance for Eastern Poland, but above all it will revive the economy of Warmia and Mazury. Based on the free access to Elbląg as a seaport, we will be able to build the entire transport chain as an element connecting north and south. The creation of this waterway, fully under Polish jurisdiction, will guarantee the freedom of shipping ships of all flags to the port of Elbląg and other ports of the Vistula River. At the same time, this investment by extending the activities of the Armed Forces of the Republic of Poland will ensure greater state security, which I place first among the priorities to implement. According to the CSO, between January and the end of July 2018, 52.4 million tons of cargo were reloaded in seaports, i.e. 21.9%. more than in the same period last year, which only shows that Polish seaports have huge potential and their development is necessary to improve the Polish economy. Thank you.

If the leading figures of the European Union continue to be guided by a manifesto from Ventotene, such an Italian island, and not by Robert Schuman's vision, if instead of respect for the will of the European nations they seek to impose their own private vision, then the long-established community will be deprived of moral and legal support. Andrzej Duda, President of the Republic of Poland, will then become the European Community for most of the people of our continent only an illusory creation dealing with imaginary problems, and real problems will be ignored or shouted out with propaganda slogans and blackmail. political correctness. Thank you. [[Applause]]
